at composeRefs.tsx:22:45                                                                                                                                                   \r\n      at Array.forEach                                                                                               \r\n      ...                                                                                                                                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  The setRef function in @radix-ui/react-compose-refs recursively triggers itself, causing React to hit the maximum update depth limit.                                          \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  In React 19, ref callback behavior changed - ref callbacks can now return cleanup functions, and React may call ref callbacks differently than in React 18. When setRef calls  \r\n  ref(value) at line 11, it triggers a re-render which invokes setRef again via the composed ref callback at line 22, creating an infinite loop.    \r\n\r\n\r\n### Expected behavior\r\n\r\n  Composed refs should work without causing infinite update loops in React 19.                                                                                                    \r\n\r\n### Reproducible example\r\n\r\nI have platejs plugins with radix popovers and radix tooltips on them. If I write fast inside plate, the page breaks. \r\nI\'m on a Macbook M1 Pro with 32 GB. One way to trigger this error is to inspect chrome and start a performance recording and start typing real fast. On Safari, for example, it doesn\'t break. I tried on restarted browser, guest mode. I\'m not the only one\r\n\r\n[CodeSandbox Template](n/a)\r\n\r\n### Suggested solution\r\n\r\n  The composeRefs and useComposedRefs functions need to handle React 19\'s new ref callback behavior. Possible approaches:                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  1. Memoize the composed ref callback to prevent unnecessary re-invocations                                                                                                     \r\n  2. Add a guard to prevent recursive setRef calls                                                                                                                               \r\n  3. Handle the cleanup function return value from React 19 ref callbacks       \r\n\r\n### Additional context\r\nRight now, it works fine with react 18 (even on next.js 16+) and we had to downgrade.\r\n\r\nI also saw this reddit post https://www.reddit.com/r/react/comments/1nnvwsp/react_19_causes_maximum_update_depth_exceeded/ where a user raised a similar case\r\n\r\nReact 19 introduced changes to ref callback behavior, including support for cleanup functions returned from ref callbacks. This may be causing the ref to be re-invoked on each render cycle.                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  Related stack trace also shows Slate editor involvement:                                                                                                                       \r\n  at {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Now I have the upstream fixed version (main branch) in hand. The key diff between the repo snapshot (1.2.4) and the upstream fix (1.2.5) in `createSlot`:\n\n**Before (buggy, in this repo):**\n```tsx\nif (!slottableElement) {\n  // ... throw or return children\n}\n\nconst slottableElementRef = getElementRef(slottableElement);\nconst composedRefs = composeRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef);   // ← new function every render\nconst mergedProps = mergeProps(slotProps, slottableElement.props ?? {});\n\nif (slottableElement.type !== React.Fragment) {\n  mergedProps.ref = forwardedRef ? composedRefs : slottableElementRef;\n}\n\nreturn React.cloneElement(slottableElement, mergedProps);\n```\n\n**After (fixed upstream):**\n```tsx\nconst slottableElementRef = slottableElement ? getElementRef(slottableElement) : undefined;\nconst composedRef = useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef);  // ← memoized, stable identity\n\nif (!slottableElement) {\n  // ... throw or return children\n}\n\nconst mergedProps = mergeProps(slotProps, slottableElement.props ?? {});\n\nif (slottableElement.type !== React.Fragment) {\n  mergedProps.ref = forwardedRef ? composedRef : slottableElementRef;\n}\n\nreturn React.cloneElement(slottableElement, mergedProps);\n```\n\nSo the fix is: use `useComposedRefs` (memoized with `useCallback`) instead of calling `composeRefs` directly on every render. This makes the composed ref callback identity stable across renders as long as `forwardedRef` and `slottableElementRef` are stable.\n\nWait — but `useComposedRefs` uses `useCallback(composeRefs(...refs), refs)` — the deps array is `refs` (the entire spread arguments). Identity is stable as long as the identity of the `forwardedRef` and `slottableElementRef` arguments doesn\'t change.\n\nHmm, but there\'s a subtle point: if `slottableElementRef` is a function prop from the user, its identity is stable unless the parent re-creates it. If it\'s a plain object ref, it\'s the same object. Good.\n\nNow, why does a new ref callback on every render cause an infinite loop in React 19 specifically (not 18)?\n\nReact 19 ref callback behavior:\n1. If a ref callback returns a function, React uses it as cleanup.\n2. **React 19 change**: When a component re-renders, if the ref callback identity changes, React calls the previous callback\'s cleanup (or the previous callback with `null`) and then calls the new ref callback with the node.\n\nIn React 18 too, a new ref callback identity causes detach + attach. So why only React 19?\n\nThe key is in React 19: the composed ref now returns a cleanup function (compose-refs 1.1.x added React 19 cleanup support — the current code in this repo already returns a cleanup when one of the underlying refs returns a function).\n\nThe mechanism of the infinite loop (from the actual React 19 behavior — I recall this specific React 19 issue):\n\nIn React 19, when a ref callback with a cleanup function is attached to a host instance, and the fiber re-renders with a new ref callback:\n- React calls the old cleanup.\n- React calls the new ref callback with the node.\n- The new ref callback (the composed one) calls the underlying refs.\n\nThat in itself is not a loop. The loop comes from **the interaction with React 19\'s "ref callback returning cleanup" and the re-rendering of the slotted element**:\n\nActually, let\'s think about the Plate/Slate scenario: each keystroke re-renders the editor tree at high frequency (50-100fps). Each render of a Radix component with asChild → Slot creates a new composed ref callback → React 19 detaches and reattaches the ref → the composed ref calls Slate\'s ref callback → Slate\'s ref callback (or a ref callback in the chain, e.g., roving focus / focus-scope, etc.) calls something that schedules a state update (e.g., focus restoration, `useCallback` with focus state) → re-render → new ref → detach/attach → … → update depth explodes.\n\nActually, more precisely, there\'s a known specific React 19 behavior: **React 19 calls a ref callback with `null` and then with the node when the ref callback changes**, and this detach/attach cycle itself can cause focus changes (blur/focus events) → state updates (e.g., Radix components tracking `hasFocused`, or Slate\'s focus tracking) → re-renders. When typing fast, each keystroke\'s re-render triggers a ref swap, and the ref swap triggers focus events + state updates → update nesting → "Maximum update depth exceeded".\n\nWhatever the exact chain of downstream updates, the root cause is the same, and it matches the upstream changelog exactly: **Slot was creating a new ref callback on every render**. Fix: memoize with `useComposedRefs`.\n\nWait, but there\'s one more thing to consider: in the current code in this repo, `composeRefs` returns a new closure every render. `useComposedRefs` memoizes with `useCallback(composeRefs(...refs), refs)`. Deps = [forwardedRef, slottableElementRef]. In the Plate case, if the user\'s ref callback is stable and forwardedRef is stable (a Radix primitive\'s forwarded ref from useComposedRefs — stable via useCallback), the composed ref is stable → React 19 doesn\'t re-invoke the ref callback on every render → no detach/attach churn → no loop.\n\nNow — the upstream fix also moved the `useComposedRefs` call to before the early return (`if (!slottableElement) { ... return children; }`). Why? Hooks must be called unconditionally before any early return. In the buggy code, `composeRefs` (not a hook) was called after the early return — no problem. But `useComposedRefs` is a hook, so it must be called before `return children;`. Upstream moved it up and guarded with `slottableElement ? getElementRef(slottableElement) : undefined`.\n\nSo the minimal fix in this repo:\n\n```tsx\n    if (slottableElement) {\n      slottableElement = React.cloneElement(slottableElement, undefined, newChildren);\n    } else if (\n      !hasSlottable &&\n      React.Children.count(children) === 1 &&\n      React.isValidElement(children)\n    ) {\n      slottableElement = children;\n    }\n\n    const slottableElementRef = slottableElement ? getElementRef(slottableElement) : undefined;\n    const composedRef = useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef);\n\n    if (!slottableElement) {\n      if (children || children === 0) {\n        throw new Error(...);\n      }\n      return children;\n    }\n\n    const mergedProps = mergeProps(slotProps, slottableElement.props ?? {});\n\n    if (slottableElement.type !== React.Fragment) {\n      mergedProps.ref = forwardedRef ? composedRef : slottableElementRef;\n    }\n\n    return React.cloneElement(slottableElement, mergedProps);\n```\n\nAnd change the import from `composeRefs` to `useComposedRefs`.\n\nHmm, wait — one more consideration: `useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` — deps array = [forwardedRef, slottableElementRef]. When `slottableElement` is null (no children), `slottableElementRef` is undefined. No problem.\n\nBut a subtle point: `useComposedRefs`\'s deps are a spread argument `...refs` — the identity of the array itself is new every render, but `useCallback` compares deps element-by-element with Object.is, so it\'s fine.\n\nNow, does this repo\'s `useComposedRefs` correctly handle the React 19 cleanup? Run `pnpm vitest run packages/react/slot` and get it green.\n\nFirst, let\'s understand how to reproduce "Maximum update depth exceeded" in a test.\n\nReproduction idea:\n- React 19 + a Slot wrapping an element whose ref callback returns a cleanup function (React 19 style).\n- Parent re-renders; each render creates a new ref callback (the buggy slot).\n- React 19: new ref identity → calls old cleanup + calls new ref(node).\n- If the ref callback (or the ref chain) triggers a state update on invocation (e.g., setting state), the update on each render... hmm, for "Maximum update depth exceeded" you need nested updates: an update scheduled during commit/ref-attach triggers a re-render that itself triggers another ref-attach update within the same nesting.\n\nHow to deterministically trigger "Maximum update depth exceeded" in a test:\n\nOption A: A ref callback that sets state (via a parent\'s callback ref). In React 19, a ref callback is called during commit. If it calls setState, React processes the update... In React 18, setState inside a ref callback is allowed (it\'s like a passive effect? no, ref callbacks are called during the commit phase, and setState there schedules an update). No — ref callbacks are not rendering; they\'re called after DOM mutation, and setState there is allowed and schedules a re-render (that\'s the old `componentDidMount`-like behavior).\n\nNested updates: render 1 → commit → ref(node) → setState → render 2 (nested) → commit → new ref identity → ref swap: cleanup + new ref(node) → setState → render 3 (nested within render 2\'s commit) → ... React throws "Maximum update depth exceeded" after about 50 nested updates.\n\nHmm wait, is that really true? "Maximum update depth exceeded" is thrown by `scheduleUpdateOnFiber` when `nestedUpdateCount > NESTED_UPDATE_LIMIT (50)`. nestedUpdateCount is incremented in `scheduleUpdateOnFiber` when `isRendering === true` (i.e., the update is scheduled during render/commit), and reset afterward. If a ref callback during commit N calls setState on the same component, and that re-render\'s commit triggers another setState in a ref callback, then each cycle increments nestedUpdateCount → after 50, throws.\n\nSo a deterministic reproduction:\n\n```tsx\nfunction Test() {\n  const [count, setCount] = React.useState(0);\n  const ref = React.useCallback((node: HTMLButtonElement | null) => {\n    // React 19 style: return a cleanup\n    return () => {};\n  }, []);\n  return (\n    <Slot ref={React.useCallback(() => setCount(c => c + 1), [])}>\n      <button ref={ref}>x</button>\n    </Slot>\n  );\n}\n```\n\nHmm, but wait: in the buggy code, the ref passed to the button is `composeRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` — a new closure every render. forwardedRef = the stable setCount callback. Yes — React 19 (and 18) re-invoke refs when the ref callback identity changes. In React 18: old ref(null), new ref(node). In React 19: old cleanup() (or old ref(null)), new ref(node).\n\nSo in the reproduction: the `forwardedRef` callback increments state on each invocation (with a node). {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'All done. Time to wrap up following the standing instructions: root cause, files changed, how verified.\n', 'thinkingSignature': 'reasoning'}, {'type': 'text', 'text': '\n\nDone. Suite green.\n\n## Summary\n\n**Root cause**\n`createSlot` in `packages/react/slot/src/slot.tsx` called `composeRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` on every render, so the ref callback attached to the slotted element got a **new function identity on every render**. React re-invokes ref callbacks whenever their identity changes (old ref detached/cleanup, new ref attached with the node). In React 19, which added ref-callback cleanup, this detach/reattach churn on every re-render is fatal for any ref in the chain that schedules work when invoked — e.g. Slate/Plate editor refs, focus tracking, state updates. Each re-render schedules another render → nested updates pile past React\'s limit of 50 → `Error: Maximum update depth exceeded` (exactly the reported stack through `setRef` in compose-refs). It "only" bit on React 19 because 19\'s ref-callback re-invocation semantics make the per-render identity churn observable; the bug is the unstable callback, independent of version.\n\nThe `compose-refs` package itself was already fixed (v1.1.2 here, upstream 1.1.1) — `useComposedRefs` (memoized via `useCallback`) was available but unused by Slot. This snapshot (HEAD = upstream #3898) predates the upstream fix PR #3899 ("Slot: Fix infinite re-render loop in React 19 due to unstable ref"), released as `@radix-ui/react-slot` 1.2.5.\n\n**Files changed**\n- `packages/react/slot/src/slot.tsx` — import `useComposedRefs` instead of `composeRefs`; compute `slottableElementRef` and `const composedRef = useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` *before* the early return (it\'s a hook, so it must run unconditionally); use the stable `composedRef` in `mergedProps.ref`. With stable deps the composed ref keeps its identity across renders, so React no longer detaches/reattaches it on re-renders.\n- `packages/react/slot/src/slot-ref-stability.test.tsx` (new) — two regression tests ported from upstream: (1) a ref callback that forces a render on attach must not loop/throw; (2) a forwardRef child must receive one stable ref identity across re-renders.\n- `.changeset/brave-foxes-listen.md` (new) — patch changeset for `@radix-ui/react-slot`, matching the upstream 1.2.5 changelog entry.\n\n**Verification**\n- Before fix: new test file fails on buggy code — test 1 throws the verbatim `Maximum update depth exceeded` error from the report; test 2 sees 3 distinct ref identities instead of 1. Old composed closure with null: `setRef(forwardedRef, null)` → forwardedRef(null); `setRef(onTriggerChange, null)` → `setTrigger(null)`. New closure with node: `forwardedRef(node)`, `setTrigger(node)`.\n  - Sequence per render: setTrigger(null) then setTrigger(node) — batching → net: trigger = node (same as before) → React bails out? With 2 setStates to the same final value in one batch: React processes setTrigger(null) (queued), then setTrigger(node) (queued) → at render time, value = node = current → Object.is equal → bail out, no re-render.\n  - So it converges: render N+1 only happens if user\'s forwardedRef does work. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Definitive: upstream released exactly the same fix for all three packages under the same changelog entry: "Fixed infinite re-render loop in React 19 caused by unstable composed ref callback references." (dropdown-menu 2.1.17, menu 2.1.17, navigation-menu 1.2.15).\n\nSo all three locations are the same bug, and upstream fixed all three. My analysis:\n- **dropdown-menu Trigger**: same bug → fix with `useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, context.triggerRef)`.\n- **menu Sub.Trigger**: same bug → fix with `useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, subContext.onTriggerChange)`.\n- **navigation-menu ViewportImpl**: same bug (upstream classifies it so) → fix by extracting a `NavigationMenuViewportItem` component that uses `useComposedRefs`.\n\nWait — regarding the navigation-menu one, my earlier analysis was that it self-converges via Object.is bail-out. But upstream classifies it as the same infinite-loop bug. Why? Let me think again…\n\n`setContent(node)` — `content` state. Hmm, when does the loop actually happen? Scenario: hover to activate item A → activeContentValue = A → item A\'s closure: `setContent(nodeA)`. Now `content = nodeA`. `handleSizeChange` reads `content`…\n\nHmm, let me think about the case where the viewport re-renders every time `activeContentValue` changes: switching active item A→B: B\'s closure: setContent(nodeB) → state change → re-render → B\'s new closure (new identity) → ref swap: old closure(null) — no-op; new closure(nodeB) → setContent(nodeB) → same value → bail out. Converges.\n\nHmm, so where\'s the infinite loop? What if `content` and the attached node were different elements… actually wait — the loop might go via `setSize`: `handleSizeChange` → `setSize({width, height})` — new object each time → re-render → new closure → ref swap → `setContent(same node)` → bail out (no loop) — but the ref swap doesn\'t trigger resize.
